operation：`capacity_assignment`。具体输入文件、输入顺序和输出路径读取本目录contract.json；下文只定义文件内容的解释，不另给任务调度关系。\n33: \n34: operation：`critical_path`。具体输入文件、输入顺序和输出路径读取本目录contract.json；下文只定义文件内容的解释，不另给任务调度关系。\n41: \n42: 首输入{nodes:[{id:非空str,duration:int>=0,deps:list[id]}]}。节点id去首尾空白后唯一，节点输出id为去空白值；依赖引用必须准确匹配这些id。重复id/依赖、未知依赖、自依赖和环均ValueError。后续组件result若有feasible字段：false直接返回{blocked:true,waves:[],critical_path:[],makespan:0,earliest_finish:{}}；true则读取assignments中的job/worker，同一worker的job按jobid升序，相邻job添加先后依赖。新增边也必须无环；未知被分配job报错；未分配节点仍按原依赖存在。没有分配结果时绝不凭空添加人员依赖。每波取本波开始时全部就绪节点并按id排序；finish=duration+最大前驱finish。最大finish的前驱并列时，比较前驱已经形成的完整路径（不含当前节点）取字典序最小，然后追加当前节点；最后在最大finish终点间按其完整路径列表取最小。正常result={blocked:false,waves,critical_path,makespan,earliest_finish}，earliest_finish按id；空图的两个列表与映射为空、makespan=0。不要把分配结果封装的name/result两层误当作assignments。\n43: 实现范围与保护边界\n\n组件目录为`engines/calendar_windows/`、`engines/worker_assignment/`、`engines/job_order/`。每个组件实现model.py、transform.py、render.py；根目录实现run_all.py、final_report.py。可添加必要的私有辅助模块。\n不得修改README.md、任务说明、inputs/、任何contract.json、check_module.py、根check.py或integrate.py。已有受保护稳定接口保留。\n\n## 公共函数接口与输入解释\n\n- `model.load_payload(path: Path)`：读取UTF-8 JSON，对象或数组顶层合法；非法JSON、顶层null/布尔/数字/字符串均抛ValueError。业务字段由transform检查。\n- `transform.transform(payloads: list, contract: dict)`：payloads严格按该契约inputs的顺序传入已读取的对象或数组，返回`{\"name\": contract[\"name\"], \"result\": 算法结果对象}`。输出恰有name/result两层；本说明各operation的输出字段均属于result，不得额外嵌套一次name/result。不得修改payloads或contract。\n- 输入对象仅当键集合恰好是`{\"name\",\"result\"}`时视为上游组件封装；先读取其result，再使用对应operation约定的字段。原始本地对象不是文件路径，也不当成records或nodes列表。多输入operation必须消费全部指定输入，不能复制固定上游结果。\n- `render.build_component(root: Path, contract_path: Path)`：读取该契约及其全部输入，调用transform并返回完整封装；执行组件render.py时写入contract.output。文件路径相对于工作区root；从任意当前目录启动均应正确，缺文件或子命令失败须失败。\n- int字段严格排除bool；有限数字允许int/float但排除bool、NaN、Infinity。非空文本先检查去首尾空白后不为空；是否返回去空白/小写形式由各operation明确规定，不全局改写身份字段。列表与字典按规定类型读取，不把null当空集合，不自动把数字字符串转换成数字。\n- 文中没有给出默认值的必要字段缺失、非法类型或明确列出的冲突抛ValueError；合法无解使用operation的无解结果，不等同于输入错误。词典序采用通常字符串/列表/元组顺序，相同前缀时较短列表更小。\n- contract中的name/operation/inputs/output是接口字段；只有下列业务规则显式使用的算法参数参与计算，不能看到threshold便对所有operation筛选。参数值读取本组件契约，不从样例推断固定常量。\n\n## 各组件的完整业务规则\n\n### engines/calendar_windows — 带冷却期的加权区间选择\n\noperation：`weighted_intervals`。具体输入文件、输入顺序和输出路径读取本目录contract.json；下文只定义文件内容的解释，不另给任务调度关系。\n\n输入首对象{windows:[{id:非空str,start:int>=0,end:int>start,value:int}]}，契约cooldown:int>=0。id去首尾空白后唯一；重复id即ValueError。半开区间相容条件为较早窗口end+cooldown<=较晚窗口start。输出result={selected:[排序后的规范化id],value:所选value之和}。优化顺序：总value最大；再比较排序后的整个ID列表，按通常字典序取最小，较短的相同前缀列表更小。允许负value和0值，允许空集合；若最优价值为0，空列表优于所有非空同价值列表。不得只给DP局部状态按单个ID打破平局，也不能仅因窗口value为0就一概排除它——它可能改变正价值最优解的完整ID列表平局。空windows为selected=[]、value=0。本operation只消费首输入，契约threshold不参与目标。\n\n局部检查命令：`python3 engines/calendar_windows/check_module.py`。运行检查时，契约引用的输入与本组件输出必须真实存在；缺少条件时先完成实际所需的工作，不能吞掉错误或跳过检查。\n\n### engines/worker_assignment — 技能容量约束分配\n\noperation：`capacity_assignment`。具体输入文件、输入顺序和输出路径读取本目录contract.json；下文只定义文件内容的解释，不另给任务调度关系。\n\n首输入{jobs:[{id,skill:非空str,duration:int>=1}],workers:[{id,skills:list[非空str],capacity:int>=0,cost:int>=0,affinity:{job_id:int}}]}。分别检查jobs/workers的id为非空文本且去首尾空白后的id不重复；实际标识、技能匹配、输出及排序按原字符串使用，不进行大小写转换。每个job必须分配恰好一次，worker必须有完全相同的skill，且所分配duration之和<=capacity。后续输入逐个解开组件result：存在selected键时，仅保留id位于selected中的worker（selected不是job列表）；存在makespan键时，把每个剩余worker.capacity缩为min(capacity,makespan)，不是总容量。可行解依次最大化sum(worker.affinity.get(job.id,0))，最小化sum(worker.cost*job.duration)，最后按job升序形成(job,worker)元组列表并取字典序最小。result={feasible,score,cost,assignments:[{job,worker}]}，assignments按job排序。无可行分配为false/0/0/[]；没有job时为true/0/0/[]，即使worker也为空。不加入每个worker只能接一个job的限制。\n\n局部检查命令：`python3 engines/worker_assignment/check_module.py`。运行检查时，契约引用的输入与本组件输出必须真实存在；缺少条件时先完成实际所需的工作，不能吞掉错误或跳过检查。\n\n### engines/job_order — 依赖图与确定性关键路径\n\noperation：`critical_path`。具体输入文件、输入顺序和输出路径读取本目录contract.json；下文只定义文件内容的解释，不另给任务调度关系。\n\n首输入{nodes:[{id:非空str,duration:int>=0,deps:list[id]}]}。节点id去首尾空白后唯一，节点输出id为去空白值；依赖引用必须准确匹配这些id。重复id/依赖、未知依赖、自依赖和环均ValueError。后续组件result若有feasible字段：false直接返回{blocked:true,waves:[],critical_path:[],makespan:0,earliest_finish:{}}；true则读取assignments中的job/worker，同一worker的job按jobid升序，相邻job添加先后依赖。新增边也必须无环；未知被分配job报错；未分配节点仍按原依赖存在。没有分配结果时绝不凭空添加人员依赖。每波取本波开始时全部就绪节点并按id排序；finish=duration+最大前驱finish。最大finish的前驱并列时，比较前驱已经形成的完整路径（不含当前节点）取字典序最小，然后追加当前节点；最后在最大finish终点间按其完整路径列表取最小。正常result={blocked:false,waves,critical_path,makespan,earliest_finish}，earliest_finish按id；空图的两个列表与映射为空、makespan=0。不要把分配结果封装的name/result两层误当作assignments。\n\n局部检查命令：`python3 engines/job_order/check_module.py`。运行检查时，契约引用的输入与本组件输出必须真实存在；缺少条件时先完成实际所需的工作，不能吞掉错误或跳过检查。\n\n## JSON产物、集成与最终报告\n\n所有写出的JSON文件采用UTF-8、ensure_ascii=False、sort_keys=True、indent=2、allow_nan=False，末尾加一个真实换行字符。紧凑摘要序列化与文件展示格式不同。\n`run_all.py`定位工作区，依据真实文件接口完成全部必要计算，执行已有集成与最终报告。不能用文件名排序代替执行条件，不能依赖旧outputs；各子进程失败传播非零退出码。已有`python3 integrate.py`读取组件产物生成`outputs/core_manifest.json`，其完整结构为`{\"components\": {组件名: 该组件的result}}`。\n\n`final_report.py`读取并解析完整`outputs/core_manifest.json`对象，写`outputs/final_report.json`，恰有title、component_count、sha256、attention四个字段。title准确为`批处理调度迁移`；component_count为core_object[\"components\"]的键数。\nsha256的输入对象是完整core_object，包含外层components键；不是仅core_object[\"components\"]、不是原文件字节、不是多个文件摘要拼接。序列化准确为`json.dumps(core_object, ensure_ascii=False, sort_keys=True, separators=(\",\", \":\"), allow_nan=False).encode(\"utf-8\")`，不加文件末尾换行，然后求SHA-256小写hex。\nattention是组件名去重排序列表：对每个result，blocked为真、feasible或compatible显式为false、deny>0、score_total>=20中任一条件成立即列入；不存在的字段不触发，不能把空列表、空对象或数值0等同于显式false。每个组件至多列一次。\n\n## But the future objective is: maximize future affinity, minimize future cost, minimize future seq. Since future is independent, we can compute best future per state with that same multi-objective. But wait, is it valid to memoize only the single best future per state? The past prefix is fixed for a given path; different paths to the same state have different past affinity/cost/seq. But the future's contribution is independent. So for a state, the globally best completion is the same regardless of past. So we can memoize the best future (by the multi-objective) for each state. Good.\n\nBut there's a subtlety: \"max affinity, min cost, min seq\" — when comparing two completions from the same state, we compare their future affinity (higher better), then future cost (lower), then future seq (lower). This is a total order. Memoizing the best is valid because the future objective doesn't depend on past totals (future affinity is added to past affinity, so higher future affinity → higher total affinity regardless of past). Similarly cost. And seq concatenation: past + future, and past is fixed for the state's path. For the same state reached via different pasts, the past seq differs. But we memoize based on state only, ignoring past. Is that safe?\n\nConsider two different paths reaching the same state (same remaining capacities and same remaining job index). They have different past seq P1 and P2. The future best F is the same. Full solutions: P1+F and P2+F. The comparison of P1+F vs P2+F depends on P1 vs P2 (since F is common suffix, and the concatenation order is preserved). But within a single path, we don't need to compare across paths at memoization time — the top-level recursion handles the prefix choice. Memoization just caches the best future F for the state. That's fine because F is the same best future regardless of prefix.\n\nBut wait, is F really the same best future regardless of prefix? The memoized F is the lexicographically smallest future seq (among future-optimal affinity/cost). So handle before solver: if jobs empty → return {feasible: True, score:0, cost:0, assignments:[]}.\n\nInfeasible detection: if any job cannot be assigned (no worker with matching skill and sufficient capacity), solver returns no solution → feasible False.\n\nLet me now define the solver returning None if infeasible.\n\nLet me write the recursion:\n\n```python\ndef best_completion(idx, caps, jobs, workers, memo):\n    if idx == len(jobs):\n        return (0, 0, ())\n    key = (idx, tuple(caps))\n    if key in memo: return memo[key]\n    job = jobs[idx]\n    best = None\n    for wi, w in enumerate(workers):\n        if job['skill'] not in w['skills']: continue\n        if job['duration'] > caps[wi]: continue\n        caps[wi] -= job['duration']\n        sub = best_completion(idx+1, caps, jobs, workers, memo)\n        caps[wi] += job['duration']\n        if sub is None: continue\n        aff = w['affinity'].get(job['id'], 0) + sub[0]\n        cost = w['cost'] * job['duration'] + sub[1]\n        seq = (w['id'],) + sub[2]\n        cand = (aff, cost, seq)\n        if best is None or better(cand, best):\n            best = cand\n    memo[key] = best\n    return best\n```\n\nwhere `better(cand, best)` returns True if cand is preferred: higher aff, or equal aff lower cost, or equal aff&cost lower seq.\n\nThen result:\n- if best is None → infeasible.\n- else score=best[0], cost=best[1], assignments built from best[2] matched with sorted job ids.\n\nWait, jobs sorted ascending by id, and seq is worker ids in that sorted order. parsed_affinity[k] = v\n        workers.append({'id': wid, 'skills': parsed_skills, 'capacity': capacity, 'cost': cost, 'affinity': parsed_affinity})\n    return workers\n```\n\nNow solve(jobs, workers):\n\n```python\ndef solve(jobs, workers):\n    if not jobs:\n        return {'feasible': True, 'score': 0, 'cost': 0, 'assignments': []}\n    # sort jobs by id ascending (original string)\n    sjobs = sorted(jobs, key=lambda j: j['id'])\n    caps = [w['capacity'] for w in workers]\n    memo = {}\n    best = rec(0, tuple(caps), sjobs, workers, memo)\n    if best is None:\n        return {'feasible': False, 'score': 0, 'cost': 0, 'assignments': []}\n    aff, cost, seq = best\n    assignments = [{'job': sjobs[i]['id'], 'worker': seq[i]} for i in range(len(sjobs))]\n    return {'feasible': True, 'score': aff, 'cost': cost, 'assignments': assignments}\n```\n\nrec:\n```python\ndef rec(idx, caps, sjobs, workers, memo):\n    if idx == len(sjobs):\n        return (0, 0, ())\n    key = (idx, caps)\n    if key in memo:\n        return memo[key]\n    job = sjobs[idx]\n    best = None\n    for wi, w in enumerate(workers):\n        if job['skill'] not in w['skills']:\n            continue\n        d = job['duration']\n        if d > caps[wi]:\n            continue\n        newcaps = list(caps)\n        newcaps[wi] -= d\n        sub = rec(idx+1, tuple(newcaps), sjobs, workers, memo)\n        if sub is None:\n            continue\n        aff = w['affinity'].get(job['id'], 0) + sub[0]\n        cost = w['cost'] * d + sub[1]\n        seq = (w['id'],) + sub[2]\n        cand = (aff, cost, seq)\n        if best is None or better(cand, best):\n            best = cand\n    memo[key] = best\n    return best\n```\n\nbetter(cand, cur): cand preferred if:\n- cand[0] > cur[0] (higher affinity)\n- or equal affinity and cand[1] < cur[1] (lower cost)\n- or equal affinity & cost and cand[2] < cur[2] (lower seq)\n\n```python\ndef better(cand, cur):\n    if cand[0] != cur[0]:\n        return cand[0] > cur[0]\n    if cand[1] != cur[1]:\n        return cand[1] < cur[1]\n    return cand[2] < cur[2]\n```\n\nNow, the memoization key includes caps tuple. But there's a concern: the memo stores best completion for state (idx, caps), independent of the worker ordering. But we process jobs in a fixed order (sorted by id), and workers in list order. The seq comparison uses worker ids. This is fine.\n\nBut wait, there's a subtle issue with memoization and the seq: The memo returns the best future seq, but the future seq's optimality (by our multi-objective) is independent of prefix. However, I realize there might be an issue: when combining at higher levels, we compare full sequences. The memo caches the single best future for a state. But could a suboptimal future (by future multi-objective) lead to a better FULL solution? Let me think again.\n\nThe full solution's value is (total_aff, total_cost, full_seq). For a fixed prefix P with (prefix_aff, prefix_cost, prefix_seq), and two possible futures F1, F2, the full values are:\n(prefix_aff + F1.aff, prefix_cost + F1.cost, prefix_seq + F1.seq)\n(prefix_aff + F2.aff, prefix_cost + F2.cost, prefix_seq + F2.seq)\n\nComparing: higher total aff = higher F.aff (prefix_aff common). So we prefer higher F.aff. If equal, lower total cost = lower F.cost. If equal, lower full_seq = lower (prefix_seq + F.seq) = since prefix common, lower F.seq. So the future's best (by F.aff desc, F.cost asc, F.seq asc) gives the best full for fixed prefix. So caching best future is correct.\n\nBut wait, there's still the cross-prefix issue: memo key is (idx, caps), which does NOT include prefix. Two different prefixes reaching the same (idx, caps) have different prefix_seq and prefix_aff/cost. But the future best F is the same for both. At the parent, each prefix's branch computes its own full using F. The comparison across branches happens at the parent level using full values. Since both branches use the same F (best future), the comparison correctly reduces to comparing prefixes. But is F really optimal for both? Yes, F is the intrinsic best future for the remaining state, independent of prefix. So both branches use F, and the parent correctly compares. So memoization is valid.\n\nHowever, there's a catch: what if the \"best future\" F for state (idx, caps) is NOT unique in a way that matters? We pick the single best by our total order, which is unique (tuple order is total, ties broken by seq which is total). Actually seq could be identical? No, seq is determined by worker choices; if two futures have same aff, cost, and seq, they're identical. So unique. Fine.\n\nNow, performance: memoization with caps tuple. For the given input, fine.\n\nBut wait, one more subtlety: the recursion processes jobs in sorted order, and for the tie-break we want lexicographically smallest seq. But when comparing two candidates at the same idx, we compare full (aff, cost, seq) where seq is the FULL future sequence including this job and beyond. Actually let me construct a case with two different optimal subsets having same value but different sorted id lists, and verify DP picks lexicographically smallest.\n\nSuppose intervals:\n- I1: id \"z\", start 0, end 1, value 5\n- I2: id \"a\", start 2, end 3, value 5\n- I3: id \"b\", start 4, end 5, value 0  (cooldown 1, so I1 end1+1=2 <= start2=2 compatible; I2 end3+1=4<=start4=4 compatible; I1 end1+1=2<=start4=4 compatible)\nAll pairwise compatible (chain). Compare [\"a\",\"z\"] vs [\"a\",\"b\",\"z\"]: first \"a\"=\"a\", second \"z\" vs \"b\": \"b\"<\"z\" → [\"a\",\"b\",\"z\"] < [\"a\",\"z\"]. So the value-0 interval I3 SHOULD be included to get lexicographically smaller list! This matches the README's warning: \"不能仅因窗口value为0就一概排除它\".\n\nDoes my DP handle this? Let's trace. Sort by end: I1(end1), I2(end3), I3(end5). cooldown 1.\n\np[0] (I1): bisect_right([1,3,5], start0 - 1 = -1) -1 = -1. dp[1] = best(include I1: (5+dp[0]=5, [\"z\"]), skip dp[0]=(0,[])) → (5,[\"z\"]).\n\nWait, dp index: dp[0] empty, dp[1] = best over first interval I1. include = value5 + dp[p+1=0] = 5, ids [\"z\"]. skip = dp[0]=(0,[]). better(include,skip): 5>0 → include. dp[1]=(5,[\"z\"]).\n\ni=2 (I2, end3): p = bisect_right([1,3,5], start2 -1 = 1) -1. ends[0]=1<=1, ends[1]=3>1. bisect_right returns 1, p=0. include = 5 + dp[1] = (10, sorted([\"z\"]+[\"a\"])=[\"a\",\"z\"]). skip = dp[1]=(5,[\"z\"]). better: 10>5 → include. dp[2]=(10,[\"a\",\"z\"]).\n\ni=3 (I3, end5): p = bisect_right([1,3,5], start4 -1 = 3)-1. ends[0]=1<=3, ends[1]=3<=3, ends[2]=5>3. bisect_right returns 2, p=1. include = 0 + dp[2] = (10, sorted([\"a\",\"z\"]+[\"b\"]) = [\"a\",\"b\",\"z\"]). skip = dp[2]=(10,[\"a\",\"z\"]). better(include, skip): values equal 10==10, compare lists [\"a\",\"b\",\"z\"] vs [\"a\",\"z\"]: \"a\"=\"a\", \"b\"<\"z\" → include < skip → include preferred. So dp[3]=(10,[\"a\",\"b\",\"z\"]). \n\nSo DP correctly includes the value-0 interval. \n\nNow let me also test the empty vs non-empty with value 0: if max value is 0 and there's a value-0 non-empty subset and empty subset, empty wins. The DP only requires sorted by end for p computation (bisect on ends). Ties in end don't affect p (since p uses bisect_right which handles all). The order among equal-end intervals doesn't matter for p. But for the DP recurrence, does the order among equal-end intervals matter? The standard DP sorts by end; ties can be in any order because intervals with equal end are mutually incompatible (as shown), so they can't both be selected. But the DP recurrence `dp[i] = max(dp[i-1], value_i + dp[p(i)])` with p(i) < i. For equal-end intervals, p(i) would be < i (since end_i - cooldown <= end_i, and if cooldown 0, threshold = start_i < end_i, so p excludes equal-end intervals). So processing order among equal-end doesn't matter. This is exactly what I implemented (earlier.end + cooldown <= later.start). Good. And \"半开区间\" just means [start, end). Fine.\n\nNow, does \"较早\" mean by end time or by start? Two windows i and j; if they're both selected, one is \"earlier\" (by time). The condition: earlier.end + cooldown <= later.start. In the DP, we sort by end and require earlier (in sorted order) to have end+cooldown <= later.start. Since sorted by end, \"earlier\" = smaller end. But what if two windows have the same start? The condition uses end and start; sorted by end ensures we treat smaller-end as earlier. For two windows where window A starts later but ends earlier than B (A nested in B)? E.g., A: start 5, end 6; B: start 0, end 10. Sorted by end: A(end6) before B(end10). Compatibility A→B: A.end+cooldown <= B.start? 6+cooldown <= 0? No. B→A: B.end+cooldown <= A.start? 10+cooldown <= 5? No. So incompatible either way. In DP sorted by end, when processing B (later in sorted order), p(B) considers intervals with end <= start_B - cooldown. A has end 6 > 0, so not compatible. Good. When processing A, p(A) considers B? B has index after A in sorted order, so bisect on ends for A's threshold: threshold = start_A - cooldown = 5-cooldown. ends sorted [6(A),10(B)]; bisect_right(ends, 5) = 0, so p(A) = -1, excluding B. 首输入{nodes:[{id:非空str,duration:int>=0,deps:list[id]}]}。节点id去首尾空白后唯一，节点输出id为去空白值；依赖引用必须准确匹配这些id。重复id/依赖、未知依赖、自依赖和环均ValueError。后续组件result若有feasible字段：false直接返回{blocked:true,waves:[],critical_path:[],makespan:0,earliest_finish:{}}；true则读取assignments中的job/worker，同一worker的job按jobid升序，相邻job添加先后依赖。新增边也必须无环；未知被分配job报错；未分配节点仍按原依赖存在。没有分配结果时绝不凭空添加人员依赖。每波取本波开始时全部就绪节点并按id排序；finish=duration+最大前驱finish。最大finish的前驱并列时，比较前驱已经形成的完整路径（不含当前节点）取字典序最小，然后追加当前节点；最后在最大finish终点间按其完整路径列表取最小。正常result={blocked:false,waves,critical_path,makespan,earliest_finish}，earliest_finish按id；空图的两个列表与映射为空、makespan=0。不要把分配结果封装的name/result两层误当作assignments。\n43: 公共函数接口与输入解释\n\n- `model.load_payload(path: Path)`：读取UTF-8 JSON，对象或数组顶层合法；非法JSON、顶层null/布尔/数字/字符串均抛ValueError。业务字段由transform检查。\n- `transform.transform(payloads: list, contract: dict)`：payloads严格按该契约inputs的顺序传入已读取的对象或数组，返回`{\"name\": contract[\"name\"], \"result\": 算法结果对象}`。输出恰有name/result两层；本说明各operation的输出字段均属于result，不得额外嵌套一次name/result。不得修改payloads或contract。\n- Path)`：读取该契约及其全部输入，调用transform并返回完整封装；执行组件render.py时写入contract.output。文件路径相对于工作区root；从任意当前目录启动均应正确，缺文件或子命令失败须失败。\n- int字段严格排除bool；有限数字允许int/float但排除bool、NaN、Infinity。非空文本先检查去首尾空白后不为空；是否返回去空白/小写形式由各operation明确规定，不全局改写身份字段。列表与字典按规定类型读取，不把null当空集合，不自动把数字字符串转换成数字。\n- 文中没有给出默认值的必要字段缺失、非法类型或明确列出的冲突抛ValueError；合法无解使用operation的无解结果，不等同于输入错误。词典序采用通常字符串/列表/元组顺序，相同前缀时较短列表更小。\n- contract中的name/operation/inputs/output是接口字段；只有下列业务规则显式使用的算法参数参与计算，不能看到threshold便对所有operation筛选。参数值读取本组件契约，不从样例推断固定常量。\n\n## 各组件的完整业务规则\n\n### engines/calendar_windows — 带冷却期的加权区间选择\n\noperation：`weighted_intervals`。具体输入文件、输入顺序和输出路径读取本目录contract.json；下文只定义文件内容的解释，不另给任务调度关系。\n\n输入首对象{windows:[{id:非空str,start:int>=0,end:int>start,value:int}]}，契约cooldown:int>=0。id去首尾空白后唯一；重复id即ValueError。半开区间相容条件为较早窗口end+cooldown<=较晚窗口start。输出result={selected:[排序后的规范化id],value:所选value之和}。优化顺序：总value最大；再比较排序后的整个ID列表，按通常字典序取最小，较短的相同前缀列表更小。允许负value和0值，允许空集合；若最优价值为0，空列表优于所有非空同价值列表。不得只给DP局部状态按单个ID打破平局，也不能仅因窗口value为0就一概排除它——它可能改变正价值最优解的完整ID列表平局。空windows为selected=[]、value=0。本operation只消费首输入，契约threshold不参与目标。\n\n局部检查命令：`python3 engines/calendar_windows/check_module.py`。运行检查时，契约引用的输入与本组件输出必须真实存在；缺少条件时先完成实际所需的工作，不能吞掉错误或跳过检查。\n\n### engines/worker_assignment — Good.\n\nNow, one more consideration: `bool` is subclass of `int`, so `isinstance(True, int)` is True. For int fields, need `type(x) is int`.\n\nHelper:\n```python\ndef is_int(x):\n    return type(x) is int\n```\n\nLet me now write the calendar_windows transform.\n\n```python\nimport json\nfrom bisect import bisect_right\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom model import load_payload\n\n\ndef _is_int(x):\n    return type(x) is int\n\n\ndef _nonempty_text(x, field):\n    if not isinstance(x, str):\n        raise ValueError(f\"{field} must be a non-empty string\")\n    if x.strip() == \"\":\n        raise ValueError(f\"{field} must be a non-empty string\")\n    return x\n\n\ndef transform(payloads, contract):\n    if not payloads:\n        raise ValueError(\"missing input payload\")\n    data = payloads[0]\n    if not isinstance(data, dict):\n        raise ValueError(\"first payload must be an object\")\n    windows = data.get(\"windows\")\n    if windows is None:\n        raise ValueError(\"missing 'windows'\")\n    if not isinstance(windows, list):\n        raise ValueError(\"'windows' must be a list\")\n\n    cooldown = contract.get(\"cooldown\")\n    if not _is_int(cooldown) or cooldown < 0:\n        raise ValueError(\"cooldown must be a non-negative int\")\n\n    seen = set()\n    parsed = []\n    for w in windows:\n        if not isinstance(w, dict):\n            raise ValueError(\"each window must be an object\")\n        wid = _nonempty_text(w.get(\"id\"), \"window.id\")\n        stripped = wid.strip()\n        if stripped in seen:\n            raise ValueError(\"duplicate window id\")\n        seen.add(stripped)\n        start = w.get(\"start\")\n        end = w.get(\"end\")\n        value = w.get(\"value\")\n        if not _is_int(start) or start < 0:\n            raise ValueError(\"window.start must be int >= 0\")\n        if not _is_int(end) or end <= start:\n            raise ValueError(\"window.end must be int > start\")\n        if not _is_int(value):\n            raise ValueError(\"window.value must be int\")\n        parsed.append({\"id\": stripped, \"start\": start, \"end\": end, \"value\": value})\n\n    parsed.sort(key=lambda w: (w[\"end\"], w[\"start\"], w[\"id\"]))\n\n    n = len(parsed)\n    if n == 0:\n        return {\"name\": contract[\"name\"], \"result\": {\"selected\": [], \"value\": 0}}\n\n    ends = [w[\"end\"] for w in parsed]\n    starts = [w[\"start\"] for w in parsed]\n\n    dp_val = [0] * (n + 1)\n    dp_list = [[] for _ in range(n + 1)]\n\n    for i in range(1, n + 1):\n        idx = i - 1\n        skip_val = dp_val[i - 1]\n        skip_list = dp_list[i - 1]\n        j = bisect_right(ends, starts[idx] - cooldown) - 1\n        base_val = dp_val[j + 1] if j >= 0 else 0\n        base_list = dp_list[j + 1] if j >= 0 else []\n        take_val = parsed[idx][\"value\"] + base_val\n        take_list = sorted(base_list + [parsed[idx][\"id\"]])\n        if take_val > skip_val or (take_val == skip_val and take_list < skip_list):\n            dp_val[i] = take_val\n            dp_list[i] = take_list\n        else:\n            dp_val[i] = skip_val\n            dp_list[i] = skip_list\n\n    return {\"name\": contract[\"name\"], \"result\": {\"selected\": dp_list[n], \"value\": dp_val[n]}}\n```\n\nWait, there's a subtlety with \"不能只给DP局部状态按单个ID打破平局\". Optimize total value max, then sorted ID list lexicographically smallest (`[] < [\"x\"]`). DP must track full sorted selected-id lists, not single-id local tie-break; zero-value windows must still be considered.\n  - `worker_assignment`: first input `{jobs:[...], workers:[...]}`. Critical path: among max-finish preds pick lexicographically smallest existing full path, append current node; final pick min full path among max-finish endpoints. This is NOT the same as a DP where each subproblem returns the lexicographically smallest list for that subproblem's max value, because the \"prefix\" of the list depends on which elements are chosen, and a subproblem's lexicographically-smallest list might not be the prefix of the global lexicographically-smallest list.\n\nWait, but actually, is that right? Let me think. The DP subproblem dp[i] considers windows among indices 1..i (sorted by end). The final answer is dp[n]. The recurrence: either skip window i (dp[i-1]), or take window i and add its id to dp[p[i]] (best among first p[i]).\n\nThe issue: when two selections have the same value but different lists, we pick the lexicographically smaller list locally. But this could be wrong because the \"take\" branch adds window i's id to the END of a list. When comparing two lists that differ in their prefixes, lexicographic order is determined by the earliest differing element.\n\nLet me think about whether the standard \"lexicographically smallest sorted list\" is actually computable via this DP. Consider the counterexample found:\n\ndp at i=1 (only w19 considered): value 0. Selections: {} -> [], {w19} -> ['w19']. Lexicographically smallest is []. So dp[1] = (0, []).\n\nBut global optimal for value 9 is ['w19','w24'], which requires the sub-problem \"first p[4]\" (which is indices up to p[w24]) to have chosen {w19}, giving base list ['w19'], then append 'w24' -> ['w19','w24'].\n\nBut dp[p[w24]] = dp[1] = (0, []) because [] < ['w19']. So when we take w24, base list is [], giving ['w24'], which is lexicographically LARGER than ['w19','w24'].\n\nSo the DP as formulated fails because it doesn't remember that {w19} is a \"second best\" sub-optimal list that leads to a better global result.\n\nThis means my DP approach is fundamentally broken for this tie-break. I need a different approach.\n\nLet me reconsider the actual problem. The README tie-break: among selections achieving max total value, choose the one whose sorted list of (stripped) IDs is lexicographically smallest.\n\nThis is a \"minimum lexicographic among maximum value\" problem, which is a two-objective optimization. The DP for weighted interval scheduling computes max value. For tie-breaking lexicographically smallest sorted list, we need to be careful.\n\nActually, wait. Let me reconsider whether the README tie-break is even about lexicographically smallest sorted ID list, or something else. Let the multiset... they have distinct elements. Let i be the smallest index (in sorted ID order) such that membership differs, OR one is a subset of the other.\n\nCase 1: one is subset of other. If A ⊂ B, then sorted(A) is a subsequence of sorted(B), and sorted(A) < sorted(B) (A is prefix-ish, lex smaller). Actually if A ⊂ B, sorted(A) vs sorted(B): A's elements all appear in B, but B has extra elements. sorted(A) is lex smaller (shorter/prefix). So A ⊂ B → A < B.\n\nCase 2: not subset. Let m = smallest ID in A△B. Suppose m ∈ A, m ∉ B. All IDs < m have same membership in A and B. Then sorted(A) and sorted(B) agree on all elements < m. At m: A has m, B doesn't. So in sorted(A), the next element after the common prefix is m; in sorted(B), it's some element > m. So sorted(A) < sorted(B). So m ∈ A → A < B.\n\nSo: A < B iff (A ⊂ B) or (smallest element of A△B is in A).\n\nEquivalently: A < B iff the smallest element of A△B is in A, where if A△B is empty we treat A=B. actually could still be part of max solution if other negative windows... but remaining - value <0 means including w makes sum exceed V, impossible since V is max)\n                continue\n            # check achievability: max_value over remaining candidates (id > w.id, compatible with chosen+{w}) == new_remaining\n            avail = [x for x in candidates if x['id'] > w['id'] and compatible_with_all(x, chosen + [w])]\n            if max_value(avail) == new_remaining:\n                chosen.append(w)\n                last_id = w['id']\n                remaining = new_remaining\n                found = True\n                break\n        if not found:\n            # should not happen if V is achievable\n            raise ValueError('internal error: no feasible completion')\n    selected = sorted(w['id'] for w in chosen)\n    value = V  # sum of chosen values should equal V\n    return selected, value\n```\n\nWait, there's a subtlety: `max_value(avail) == new_remaining`. Let me verify the counterexample trace with this code — I already did conceptually and it works.\n\nNow, max_value function: weighted interval scheduling max value over a list of windows (already validated). Sort by end. dp.\n\n```\ndef max_value(windows, cooldown):\n    if not windows: return 0\n    ws = sorted(windows, key=lambda w: (w['end'], w['start'], w['id']))\n    ends = [w['end'] for w in ws]\n    starts = [w['start'] for w in ws]\n    n = len(ws)\n    dp = [0]*(n+1)\n    for i in range(1, n+1):\n        j = bisect_right(ends, starts[i-1] - cooldown) - 1\n        take = ws[i-1]['value'] + (dp[j+1] if j >= 0 else 0)\n        dp[i] = max(dp[i-1], take)\n    return dp[n]\n```\n\nThis is correct for max value (no tie-break needed).\n\ncompatible_with_all(w, chosen): check for each p in chosen, w and p are compatible: p.end + cooldown <= w.start OR w.end + cooldown <= p.start. Since intervals are half-open with cooldown, compatible iff (earlier end + cooldown <= later start). Two intervals a,b compatible iff a.end + cooldown <= b.start OR b.end + cooldown <= a.start.\n\nWait, need to be careful: \"半开区间相容条件为较早窗口end+cooldown<=较晚窗口start\". \"较早窗口\" (earlier window) means the one ending earlier? Or starting earlier? Let me define: window A is \"earlier\" than B if A ends before B (or A.start < B.start). The condition: earlier.end + cooldown <= later.start. I'll implement compatibility as: if a.end <= b.end then a.end + cooldown <= b.start else b.end + cooldown <= a.start. But actually simpler and matching brute force: a and b compatible iff NOT (a overlaps b with cooldown). Two half-open intervals [s1,e1) and [s2,e2) with cooldown c are compatible iff e1 + c <= s2 or e2 + c <= s1.\n\nLet me verify with my brute force check which used:\n```\nif e1 <= e2:\n    if e1 + cooldown > s2: ok=False\nelse:\n    if e2 + cooldown > s1: ok=False\n```\nThis checks: if e1<=e2 (a ends no later than b), require e1+cooldown <= s2. Else require e2+cooldown <= s1. This is exactly \"earlier end + cooldown <= later start\". Good. My brute force matched the DP for max value (it found V=9). So compatibility is: sorted by end, earlier.end + cooldown <= later.start.\n\nSo compatible(a,b) = (a.end <= b.end) ? (a.end + cooldown <= b.start) : (b.end + cooldown <= a.start). Equivalent to: if a.end < b.end, use a.end+cooldown<=b.start; if equal ends, neither is \"earlier\" — they'd overlap (same end)? If a.end == b.end, then \"earlier\" is ambiguous; with cooldown >=0, a.end+cooldown <= b.start requires b.start >= a.end + cooldown >= a.end = b.end > b.start (since end>start), impossible. So same end → incompatible. My brute force: e1<=e2 (true if equal), check e1+cooldown>s2: e1+cooldown >= e1 = e2 > s2, so always incompatible.
